## Compression plateau

### Promotion repair triage, 2026-10-10

The preserved check has 30 failures. Its first assertions identify 23 snapshot
failures and seven property failures. Auditing the later assertions finds four
more property failures inside those snapshot cases: an opaque family byte
ceiling, two progressive byte ceilings and an optional-map refusal count. The
complete triage is 19 snapshot-only cases and 11 cases requiring encoder or
allocation-boundary repair. Snapshot updates follow successful property checks
and independent decoding.
Input hashes, alpha, coverage, cancellation and allocation cleanup remain fixed.

| Snapshot case | Reason its exact output may change |
| --- | --- |
| Artwork, optional exact-search storage limit | Shared opaque color selection changes the bounded VarDCT fallback. |
| Artwork, progressive | Shared opaque quantization changes progressive color reconstruction. |
| Artwork, 1025x1025 | Visible-color routing replaces the historical artwork area ceiling. |
| Artwork, 512x512 | Shared opaque color selection changes the prior small-image stream. |
| Cone-selection photo | Estimated order/family selection changes serialization and shared color decisions. |
| Fine-selection photo | Shared opaque photo quantization changes the pinned file and decoded digest. |
| Fine quantizer scratch fallback | Shared opaque policy changes the preceding stream while preserving recovery. |
| DC, large distance-4 gradient | E4 order/family selection changes the retained photo stream. |
| DC, normal fine gradient | Shared opaque fine quantization changes the normal photo stream. |
| DC, small textured gradient | The photo bundle now applies according to content instead of image area. |
| DC, synchronous optional DC failure | Shared color quantization changes the bounded preceding stream. |
| DC, asynchronous optional DC failure | Cooperative output follows the same shared policy as synchronous output. |
| Flat palette, ordinary graphic | Content routing may choose a smaller exact candidate with lower error. |
| Flat palette, constrained storage | Shared color quantization changes the fallback while its error bound stays. |
| Large blocks, 129x65 | Geometry-qualified photo tools replace area-based selection. |
| Large blocks, 257x129 | Geometry-qualified photo tools replace area-based selection. |
| Large blocks, minimum working budget | Serializer deduplication changes exact output and peak accounting. |
| Large blocks, progressive | Shared opaque color quantization changes the progressive stream. |
| Coefficient order, opaque nonprogressive | Estimated order selection changes serialization under shared color policy. |

| Property case | Required action |
| --- | --- |
| Thin 2065x17 image | Resolved in LL-056: error 1.35032 below the unchanged 1.5 limit, exact alpha. |
| Thin 17x2065 image | Resolved in LL-056: error 1.29603 below the unchanged 1.5 limit, exact alpha. |
| Fine distance-0.54 rate-map failure | Resolved in LL-054: error 1.57781 below the unchanged 1.6 limit, exact alpha and closed ownership. |
| Exact palette winner | Resolved in LL-057: original 2,262-byte stream and every original sample remain exact. |
| Contrast boundary, nonprogressive | Resolved in LL-058: unchanged RMSE change below 0.1 and contrast change below 0.05, including both distance boundaries. |
| Family-context allocation fallback | Resolved in LL-059: release dead raw histograms before optional DC/alpha searches; retain the unchanged 17,200-byte ceiling. |
| Insufficient working budget | Resolved in LL-060: measured adjacent rejection at 797,197 bytes and success at 797,198 after removing 64 bytes of scratch; LIMIT and ownership assertions stay fixed. |
| Family contexts, opaque nonprogressive | LL-057 restores the exact 2,262-byte winner within the unchanged 2,316-byte ceiling; full grids qualified in LL-064 and its original digest is unchanged. |
| Family contexts, opaque progressive | LL-062 restores 8,153 bytes below the unchanged 8,247-byte ceiling; full grids qualified in LL-064. |
| Coefficient order, opaque progressive | LL-062 restores 8,016 bytes below the unchanged 8,100-byte ceiling; full grids qualified in LL-064. |
| DC, optional filter-map failure | LL-063 restores exactly one refusal and error 2.27846 below the unchanged 2.43 bound; full grids qualified in LL-064. |

No property threshold or original sample expectation has been weakened. The
seven property cases were unresolved at the preservation checkpoint. LL-054
resolves the fine-map fallback, LL-056 resolves both thin orientations, LL-057 resolves the exact palette winner, LL-058 resolves continuity, LL-059 resolves family allocation fallback, and LL-060 tightens the actual insufficient-budget boundary. LL-062 and LL-063 restore the additional progressive and optional-map mechanisms. All remaining protected byte limits and complete preceding-stream comparisons must pass before promotion.

The compression trials since E3 have not produced a retained 1% development
BD-rate improvement. The speed improvements in LL-036 and LL-044 remain valuable,
but do not close either Butteraugli target. This reaches the stop condition in
section 9 of [LOSSY_STEERING.md](LOSSY_STEERING.md). No further compression trial
is running. The retained working changes and all measured evidence are preserved.

The remaining approaches are ranked by their likely usefulness, without assigning
an unmeasured percentage gain:

1. Diagnose im26-3000's repeated final-quality regression using the held shared
   reconstruction rows. Local selector error has repeatedly failed to protect this
   development image. This has the strongest evidence for explaining the failures.
2. Test one precision-refinement pass against regional error in final reconstructed
   pixels. This could address the broader Butteraugli gap, but requires a model
   that protects every color channel and fits the effort-7 time budget.
3. Replace expensive complete-candidate searches on originals with cheaper
   selection. This addresses the unqualified 5x original speed target; the palette
   precheck only applies at up to 1 MP and cannot solve that target.

Holdout, watch-original qualification, successful full checks on the resumed
source, and a commit remain pending. The resumed full check fails as recorded
below. E1/E2 still have protected-image regressions against the unchanged
baseline, so the combined working configuration is not promoted.

The first original preparation pass made nine watch PNGs. `prior-12mp-im26-1416`
failed because our baseline JPEG reader leaves a terminal RST3 before EOI
unconsumed. This is recorded as a preparation failure, not a missing photo or a
multiscan source. The two hashed originals used by baseline speed preparation
succeeded. The full ten-original watch run cannot qualify until this is fixed.

The resumed preparation pass now decodes that terminal-restart JPEG and prepares
all ten watch originals, plus both separate 2080-square scale crops, without
failures. The ordinary JPEG regression covers buffered and streaming decode,
strict and tolerant modes, marker validation, and browser execution.

## Resumed checkpoint validation

The resumed source passes the generated documentation, package-size, strict
TypeScript, browser-build, lint and format gates. The full test suite reports
3,824 passed, 30 failed and three skipped tests across 288 passed, eight failed
and one skipped file. The run finishes in 16m58.89s with a 2.9 GiB cgroup peak
and zero swap. Both focused Chromium tests pass, covering 30 JPEG marker cases
and six JPEG XL RGB/RGBA cases.

Direct fixture diagnostics expose four quality problem areas in the held
configuration:

- The fine-quality 4 MP allocation fallback has mean color error 1.84021,
  above its existing 1.6 limit. The normal fine-quality path passes at 1.57781.
- The 2065x17 and 17x2065 fixtures have mean color errors 1.57784 and 1.50887,
  above their existing 1.5 limit.
- The nonprogressive contrast fixture changes RMSE by 0.67536 across distances
  3.99 and 4.01, above its existing 0.1 limit. Contrast is nearly zero on both
  sides, while the progressive control retains substantial texture.
- The exact-palette fixture now loses color samples, with maximum color error
  four and a 9,500-byte file. Its historical exact file is 2,262 bytes.

All seven DC diagnostics and 27 small-fixture diagnostics complete their helper
layout and ownership checks. Completion does not replace the quality limits
above. Alpha stays exact in these diagnostics. The ordinary flat-palette case
retains every sample, and its constrained fallback maximum error is seven,
within its existing limit of eleven. The formerly insufficient large-block
budget now succeeds below its cap. File, pixel and budget expectations have not
been rewritten to hide these changes.

Eleven small-fixture results, including both thin-image failures and all eight
contrast boundary/mode points, are identical in a control that replaces only
the current VarDCT and coefficient writer with their saved pre-cleanup versions.
The fine-quality allocation fallback also has identical complete encoded bytes
and ownership accounting in that control. Other dependencies remain current;
these controls cover the two encoder-module cleanups, not a complete earlier
checkout. Results and logs are retained under
`.tmp/jpegxl-lossy-lab/checkpoint-bundle-cleanup/`.

Aaron resumed candidate promotion repairs on 2026-10-10. The preservation
checkpoint is committed and pushed as `e0241edb` to `jxl-lossy-candidate`; main requires
all promotion gates. New compression trials remain deferred until promotion.

## Earlier stopping checkpoint, before owner feedback

All 64 development crops are prepared. The encoder gains at `7230f091` are
preserved, and this lab checkpoint makes no production encoder changes.
The pilot, screen, cached peer curves and 505 compressed outputs are retained in
`.tmp/jpegxl-lossy-lab/`. No holdout measurements or encoder experiments were run.
The isolated speed run was stopped after 138 of 198 jobs, covering all three
engines on 46 development crops. Its results are explicitly partial.

The latest cleanup removed 138 reproducible decoded PNGs (97.80 MiB), after
archiving 197 metadata files and the preparation and speed logs. Every prepared
input, measured JPEG XL file, score and peer cache remains present.
The previous checkpoint removed another 367 decoded PNGs (425.09 MiB).
Metadata, run logs and deletion records are saved under
`.tmp/jpegxl-stop-cleanup-2026-10-09/paused-lab/`.

plus three existing test skips and one skipped file. Build, browser, type,
documentation, package, lint and formatting checks passed. The test suite took
791.96 seconds. The check log is saved with the cleanup records.

Predictor validation plan after LL-995: use fixed overlapping 16/32/64 windows in display and linear luma, four independent error features (RMSE, variance-normalized error, source-gradient-normalized error, contrast amplitude error), max and third-norm pooling. No native peak location or fitted weights. Diagnose on the 16 screen photos at d1.3/1.85/3, then check the unchanged formulas on the remaining 48 development photos. This is not holdout access. A useful sign predictor must reach 80% accuracy on at least 10 changes above 0.1% and never predict safety for native harm above 1%. These are diagnostic acceptance criteria, not replacements for the normal lab gates. Postdecode features are an optimistic prerequisite; an encoder-side approximation would still need its own validation and speed measurement.

Final-model entropy plus real metadata gets 13/13 signs, mean error 7.255 bits, max 20.151. | Instrumentation uses extra full token walks and writes; timings excluded. | Final-model batch scoring is credible, but a frozen model alone cannot authorize changes or encode unseen contexts. LL-1025 plan: test stronger own-source alternatives using the validated direct renderer and adaptive rate query. Rank aligned 64-pixel tiles by the retained reconstruction's maximum perceptual error. Compare control with four alternatives: increase AC precision by 25% in the top one or top four tiles; replace the top tile's transforms with DCT8; combine that replacement with 25% higher AC precision. Keep DC steps, chroma maps and filters fixed, but recompute the transform-dependent DC coefficients and AC coefficients from source pixels. Clone DC storage so alternatives cannot mutate control. Verify an unchanged rebuild and trial reconstruction against the finished-stream decoder before the 80-row screen. Select using the prior lambda-1 log-rate/mean-log-error cost, 32-bit rate margin and 1% predicted harm guard. Serialize only the selected geometry. This measures available quality gain; full perceptual scratch remains diagnostic and does not pass production speed or memory gates. No peer decisions or holdout access. Raw root: joint-allocation-20261028.

LL-1026 plan: port and test one first-iteration update from libjxl's reconstruction-driven quantization loop. Aggregate our complete dithered perceptual map by selected transform using the source 1.2-scaled 16th norm. Keep the source 0.2 low-error exponent, high-error linear precision increase and range/asymmetry constraints. Adapt to fixed raw quantization units, global scale and DC steps. Target the requested distance, recompute coefficients once and serialize once, without alternative-stream or native-label selection. This logic runs above effort 7 in libjxl; it must earn its own quality/speed tradeoff here. Record attribution, validate small numeric fixtures and screen 80 rows. The validated complete model is new evidence that justifies this direct trial after the earlier effort audit. No holdout access.

LL-1043 plan, second of three: give Gaussian convolution an optional existing destination. Reuse LF bands, the blue MF plane, UHF scratch and activity storage at points where original values are no longer needed. Horizontal convolution completes before vertical writes, allowing safe input/output aliasing. Remove discarded result planes and copies without caching extra state or changing arithmetic. Require exact prepared planes and maps, source ownership/aliasing fixtures, and incremental timing against LL-1042. Full-map allocation work is preferable here to Gaborish work shared with the control, which would not directly reduce the new policy's relative cost.

Load one new value per tap and shift scalar locals, preserving each output's term order. After LL-1048, the refreshed three-encode warm profile puts blur at 9.502% of total self samples. Vertical multiply/adds and their tap/index traversal now dominate its remaining sampled lines; horizontal work is concentrated in the tap loop and the one new input load.

LL-1049 plan, third and final change: traverse one contiguous vertical input row per tap and accumulate into the existing output row, followed by the same normalization. This shares each coefficient and row lookup across the whole row instead of eight outputs. It trades register accumulators for cache-local output traffic without new storage. Require exact scalar-reference, alias, prepared-plane and full-map results; compare against LL-1048 and revert if time does not improve. No fourth change.
